LANDLORDS AND THE-IR SCHOOLS.

A letter was read from Mr. Cecil J. Parker, agent for the Duke of Westminster, relative to the Duke's inten- tion of resuming possession of certain school buildings on his land if the Education Bill were passed the Duke would secure, by arrangement either with the local education authority or otherwise, that no break should take place in the education of the children concerned, as he does not wish the children to suffer in any way on account of any action of Parliament. A similar letter was received from Mr. W. P. White, of Mere Hall, with regard to More School. It was decided to ask Lord Egerton (who had given the Committee legal notice to give up the schools without any reservation) to give the Committee the same undertaking as the Duke of Westminster—that he would continue the use to the local authority of the schools until the authority I can provide other accommodation.

WIRRAL AND BIRKENEIE AD AGRICULTURAL SOCIRI'Y'S SHOW. The Prize Schedules for the 65th Annual Show which is to be held on the 15th and 16th July, in the Society's beautiful Show Grounds at Bebington have now bean I issued. Sir Gilbert Greenall Bart. is the President of I the Society this year. The Council has again previclel a most extensive classification, also a generous list of Prizes totalling some £3,000, further the Society's valuable Challenge Bowls in addition to numerous other special prizes are offered for competition in the various sections of the Show. The classification for Horses is one of the most extensive ever issued and includes Coach Four-in-Hand, and Tandem Teams, also Pairs and Lespers in addition to many classes for Saddle Horses, and Single Harness Turnouts. New Classes forTradesnasn's Turnouts open to the Society's District (which includes all parts within a radius of 25 miles from the Show Yard), are also included in this years list of classes. The Coach Hornblowing Competitions will again take place on the first day of the Show. Channel Island and Kerry Cattle are also again pro- vided for this year, and the Valuable Challenge Bowls and also several Special Prizes are offered for Dairy Produce. Dogs, Poultry, Pigeons, Rabbits and Cavies are extremely well provided for, and the Kennel Club has again favoured this old-established Society by selecting it to rank as a Championship Show, and offering some thirty-two Challenge Prizes for Champion Dogs, the competition for same being open to the World. Two Silver Cups each valued at Five Guineas will be given for the respective best sporting and non-sporting Dog or Bitch. Any of the above named exhibits consigned to Bebington station by passenger train, will be carted to and from the show free. Lectures on bees and honey along with demonstra- tions will he given each day in addition to which there will be a Grand Naval amd Military Tournament in the Horse Judging Ring each evening after the Leaping Competitions have concluded. Prizes being offered for the various competitions, .which will be governed by the same conditions as the London Royal Military Tournament. COUNTY PALATINE OF CHESTER. NOTICE IS HEREBY GIVEN that the next GENERAL QUARTER SESSIONS of the Peace for the COUNTY PALATINE OF CHESTER will be holden at the CASTLE OF CHESTER, in and for the said County, on MONDAY, the 29th day of June, 1908, at half-past Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon, when the Grand Jury will be empannelled. The bearing of all Appeals arising within the Petty Sessional Divisions of Broxton, Chester Castle, Dares- bury, Eddisbury, Nantwich, Runcorn, Winston], and Wirral, forming the Chester District, and the trials of all prisoners committed from the last-mentioned Divisions, will take place at such Sessions. The Grand and Petty Juries for the Chester D:strict must be in attendance at the Castle of Chester, at the Opening of the Court on MONDAY, the 29th day of June, 1908, at Half-past Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. THE COURT will be adjourned to the COURT HOUSE, KNUTSFORD, on WEDNESDAY, the Ist day of July, 1908, at Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. The bearing of all Appeals arising within the Petty Sessional Divisions of Altrincham, Bucklovv, Dukin- field, Middlewich, Sandbach, Congleton, Northwich, Prestbury and Stockport, foiming the Knutsford Dis- trict, and the trials of all prisoners committed from such Divisions will take place at such Adjourned Sessions. The 'Grand and Petty Juries for the Knots- ford District must be in attendance at the Court House, Knutsford, at the opening of the Conrt on WEDNESDAY the Ist day of July nest, at Eleven o'clock in the Forenoon. REGINALD POTTS, Clerk of the Peace for the County of Chester. Chester, 9th June, 1008.

TRADES AND LABOUR COUNCIL. The Rate of Wages at Wlnwlek Asylum The monthly meeting of the Warrington Trades and Labour Council was held in the Independent Labour Party rooms on Tuesday evening, the president (Mr. W. H. Richardson) in the chair. At the last meeting a resolution was passed opposing the conversion of the Royal London Friendly Society into a Mutual Insurance Society. The CHAIRMAN said that if he understood Mr. Moorfleld aright, ha told them it was going to he converted into a limited company. Mr. MOOR VIELD : A mutual company. The CHAIRMAN : I understood a limited company. Continuing the chairman said he had heard a good deal about the matter and he did not think they were justified in passing the resolution,because from what he could see —of course, he was not an insurance agent and did not know as much about it as Mr. Moorfleld—he did not think it would be detrimental to the interests of the members in converting the society into a mutual society. Neither would it be detrimental to the agents. As far as he could see the agents would be inn better rosition. Mr. MOORFIELD said the agents were against it. It was a step in the right direction in passing that resolu- tion. The Council passed on to the next business. THE HOURS OF LABOUR. The SECRETARY (Mr. E. Plinston) read a letter from the Bolton Trades and Labour Counoil, which stated that Mr. Gill, M.P., had charge of a Bill which was now before Parlinment to abolish the system of one man being in charge of two horses and two carts, and to otherwise regulate the hours of labour. They asked the Warrington Trades Council to forward a resolution to their Member, asking him to push forward the Bill to the best of his ability. Mr. PLUMB moved that that course be adopted. Mr. PODMORE seconded and the motion was carried. THE WAGES QUESTION. Councillor STOTT said an effort had been made to persuade the Asylums Board to adopt Trades Union rate of wages with regard to all men employed at Winwick Asylum. An important factor had been decided, and that was that men temporarily employed at the as lam were now paid Trades Union wages, and worked under Trades Union conditions.' The full Trades Union rate of wages would not be paid to the permanent staff. In many instances a very large increase of wages bad been secured by these alterations. (Applause.) The CHAIRMAN said they were all very pleased to see Councillor Stott with them that night. (Hear, bear.) He moved a vote of thanks to him for hi 3 presence and his words. There was a chorus of seconders and the motion was carried unanimously. VACANCIES. Owing to Councillor Stott and Mr. H. Ifigginbotom leaving the:Council, Messrs. Moorfield end Owen were appointed in their places on tha Executive Committee of the Council.

Tunstall and Co., Stock and Sher°, t Brokers, Bewsey ()bombers, Warrington, and will be found reliable. Death of Mr. W. Perkins. Many of our readers will regret to hear of the, death of Mr. William Perkins, formerly of Warring- ton, who passed away at his residence in Bondgate, Bishop Auckland, early on Tueed ty m arning at the early age of 33 years. Mr. Perkins' early days were- speat in Warrington and he was articled as an archi- tect to the late Mr. J. E. Wright. He left here it dozen or fifteen years ago to take up an appointment as assistant surveyor to the Auckland Rural Council. In a few years he commenced business as an archi- tect and surveyor and made rapid strides. He designed two large hospitals for the local Joint Hospital Board and was very successful in snbmitting competitive plans in connection with different schemes in various parts of the country. He was for several years a useful member of the Urban Council and came to ha chair man the year before last -an important year in view* of the Royal visit in connection with the Volunteer bazaar. Daring his chairmanship year he also officiated as a Justice of the Peace. Mr. Perkins was generous-hearted and gave liberally in many direction lie provided the trophy known as the Perkins Cup, to be competed for by members of the Auckland Rills Club. Ho was a Past Master of the Wear Valley Lodge of Freemasons, and was prominently identified with other organizations. A. number of years al° Mr. Perkins met with a cycling accident. go was most seriously injured and, in fact, he lost the' use of one eye. His recovery from the accident was slow and was probably never complete. The funeral took place at Auckland cemetery on Thursday in the' presence of a large gathering, including local' Freernasons,members of the Auckland Urban Councils. and prominent Bishop Auckland townsmen.
